    Zosia has not heard a word the Chief has said all briefing and as Lieutenant, she is disappointed in herself for letting her focus slip the way it has. She hopes that Carol has been listening enough for the both of them.

    By the time the briefing is over, there is no time to dwell. Several calls come in and Jeff and Rick are eager to get on the first one. Manousos and Koumba descend to the evidence locker, and Zosia, brain foggy, heads to the break room with her now cold coffee as Carol hangs back with the Chief to split up case files.

    The moment Zosia pulls the microwave door open, the ungodly stale leftover fumes from Margaret’s Friday afternoon fish assault smack her in the face and she gags and slams the door shut.

    “Oh fuck you, Margaret,” she curses under her breath, rubbing the crease between her brows. She’ll just drink her coffee cold then.

    Shortly after, Zosia hears familiar footsteps approaching. She exhales knowing exactly who it is and Carol enters holding a stack of files.

    “There you are,” she says walking up to Zosia. She sets the files down and leans up against the counter in front of her. She tugs once at the badge around her neck and Zosia can’t help but smile as she stumbles forward.

    She has every intention to lean in and kiss Carol on the cheek but then she notices just how thick the stack of files is and she frowns.

    “How many more can there possibly be?”

    “You don’t wanna know,” Carol offers, matching her sentiment.

    Zosia sighs, already mentally preparing for another long night. She looks at Carol, whose hand is still gripped around her badge and just wants to kiss her but as she tries again, the sound of many footsteps and voices fill the hallway and they startle back. And good, too, because four uniformed officers enter and take over the space, chatting loudly as if they don’t even see the two of them.

    Zosia takes it as their cue to leave, so they leave and Monday just becomes another Monday

    —

    Tuesday feels different. Zosia can’t exactly pinpoint it. Maybe it’s because Jeff is acting entirely too normal and if Zosia knows anything about Jeff, it is that he is very much not normal. He is just as weird as Rick is but in a kind of Yin and Yang way, which is why they make perfect partners.

    See, if Jeff knows, Zosia wants him to know. If he doesn’t know, she wants certainty. But instead what she gets is normalcy threaded with ambiguity and that makes her suspicious and on edge.

    The man greets her in the morning the way he usually does. Friendly, asks about work related items, makes a joke at Rick’s expense about his goofy looking suspenders. Then he spends half his day at his desk buried in paperwork without another word to Zosia. And yet, somehow, Zosia catches him twice watching her. Not her and Carol, just her. It is something she cannot shake because it is one of those experienced detective looks that one would develop after decades of observing people. It’s a look that says he has noticed something and that makes Zosia’s stomach twist because of course, there is in fact something to notice and there has been for a long time.

    Later that afternoon, she is standing next to Carol in front of the evidence board in the bullpen reviewing photographs from a convenience store break in. As Carol leans in and points up at something, she brushes so close against Zosia that Zosia can smell her shampoo and she does not even realize it when she places her hand on the small of Carol’s back until Carol momentarily stiffens and shoots her a quick glance.

    Zosia’s hand flies off her but the boundary did not slip because of carelessness, it slips because being near Carol has become natural. When she looks around, she might notice Koumba staring at them but they both look away at the same time.

    Then a little bit later while they are going cross-eyed from staring at the same photos, Zosia makes a quiet joke about how the Cowboy Suite should have come with a mechanical bull instead of a pool table and Carol laughs. Not some professional, restrained laugh, but a warm and bright giggle that fills the air like Zosia’s favourite song. It’s the kind of laugh that Zosia had spent weeks secretly trying to earn before she could finally admit her feelings.

    Without thinking, she smiles back just as warmly and for a split second, the rest of the room disappears until a phone rings loudly and both of their smiles vanish and they correct themselves with a speed that is actually comical.

    But it leaves Zosia with a sinking feeling because as she glances around again, they have caught the attention of a few more prying eyes and now she is not sure that if anyone were to really look at them, would they believe that there is nothing there?

    She certainly would not.

    —

    On Wednesday, it happens during lunch. The break room is full. Zosia is sitting next to Carol while they not so inconspicuously eat the exact same lunch that Carol had packed for the both of them - penne pasta, roasted broccoli, and cubed mango all in identical containers.

    Across the table, Rick is talking loudly and that alone should have been the first warning because Rick has a very unique talent for accidentally saying exactly the wrong thing at exactly the wrong time. And for some reason today, the entire fucking department decides to take lunch at the same goddamn time.

    “…oh yeah,” Rick interrupts Margaret, rice flinging into the air as he points to Carol and Zosia with his fork. “That’s like when I saw these two at the farmer’s market a couple weeks ago. What a nice surprise.”

    Zosia stops mid-chew and her stomach drops straight to the floor, face shading red before she even knows it. Beside her, she can feel the way Carol goes deathly still and the way the chatter quiets around them. From her peripheral, as she glares straight into Rick’s soul wishing her eyes could shoot lasers, she can see the way few heads pop up and turn in their direction.

    Rick, stupid oblivious motherfucker, keeps going. “Gosh, Lieutenant, you had that giant husk of corn and Carol you had all those chiles and tomatoes. Looked like you guys had quite the morning. I was waiting for my dinner invite!”

    Truly, if she could, Zosia would evaporate him immediately on the spot, laser his head clean off his shoulders. From a few seats over, Manousos snorts knowingly, Margaret mumbles something about loving the farmer’s market and whines that she wishes she had someone to go with, and a few others simply just chuckle.

    “People go to the farmer’s market, Rick.” Zosia says it though she’s not entirely sure if she has blacked out or not. “It’s not a big deal.”

    But it is a big deal.

    Really, that should be it. The conversation should end here, but instead Zosia becomes painfully aware of every single eye that has shifted towards them and the memory of that morning floods back. The way they had spent hours wandering around with absolutely nowhere else to be. The way she had felt, and still feels, happier than she has in years.

    At the time, she had not been thinking about colleagues, or consequences, or who might see them. She had just been enjoying being with Carol and now she understands how that might have looked if anyone other than Rick had seen them. They looked like a couple; they had been a couple even if they had not yet discussed a label.

    The love was already there.

    Against her better judgement, she glances toward Jeff and he is already looking at her. In that moment when their eyes meet, Zosia experiences this deeply unpleasant feeling that whether or not he has spoken to Lucas, he already knows exactly what is going on and has likely known for a while, and he is definitely not the only one.

    Suddenly, the implications of hers and Carol’s careers flash before her.

    Eventually, Jeff looks back down his cold pizza and Koumba, who thankfully picks up on the tension, loudly changes the subject and asks who is going to trivia night at the bar later. Finally, the conversation moves on Zosia finishes her lunch even though she might not be able to taste it anymore.

    —

    By Thursday evening, this strain on Zosia becomes impossible to ignore as she sits alone and silently in her Explorer, engine idling in the parking lot. It’s not because anyone has been talking (and if they are, she does not know), nobody has even confronted her. Not yet, anyways.

    It’s because she’s too self-aware now of how much energy she is spending trying to not love Carol in public and it is exhausting.

    Every instinct Zosia has pulls her towards Carol and every day there are dozens of tiny moments that require conscious correction. There is the urge to always sit beside her in meetings, the instinct to look at her first when literally anything happens. And then of course there is the stupid smile that always betrays her whenever Carol walks into a room.

    Zosia hadn’t realized that loving someone could feel so amazing but also like this.

    So by the time she arrives at Carol’s house for dinner, Chinese takeout in hand, she already knows what she needs to say.

    Dinner comes and goes. They sit at the dining room table while they actively do not talk about work, and then they do the dishes and clean up the kitchen together. The simple domesticity of it is nearly enough to derail Zosia’s resolve.

    After the final dish gets dried and placed back into the cupboard, they migrate to the couch where Carol flips on the TV and an old episode of Parks and Rec is already playing, but after a few minutes of Zosia not hearing a thing that Leslie Knope is saying, she reaches for the remote and mutes the TV.

    Immediately Carol looks over and all it takes is that one look for her to know that something is on Zosia’s mind and has been for a while.

    “Hey, Zosh, what’s wrong?”

    Zosia looks down her hands and huffs out a quiet breath. She didn’t think this would be so hard. “I need to tell you something as your Lieutenant and not your girlfriend.”

    Carol’s expression changes and softens, almost like she has been waiting for this conversation.

    “And I really, really need you to not panic.”

    Carol nods slowly and the look she gives Zosia makes her chest ache because she knows that Carol already knows. Perhaps not the exact words, but the feeling of inevitability - she has known just as long as Zosia but they have danced around it out of fear and maybe slight delusion.

    “We have to tell Schrader about us.”

    The silence that follows feels enormous and suffocating, like a thousand-pound blanket resting on Zosia’s shoulders and she braces herself, waiting for that fear to creep into Carol’s eyes, preparing for their potential first argument and for Carol to point out everything they stand to lose, like their careers and reputations.

    Instead, Carol simply looks down and grabs Zosia’s hands and looks back up and nods.

    “Yeah,” she says. “I think it’s time.”

    The relief is overwhelming to the point of pain and for a few seconds, Zosia cannot speak. She had not realized how much she needed to hear Carol agree until this very moment. But it is clear that they both understand exactly the implications of Zosia’s words

    “Yeah?” Zosia repeats. “Really?”

    Carol nods again, flashing Zosia half a smile and Zosia reaches a hand up and touches Carol’s face, her thumb grazing along her jawline.

    “So what do you think happens?” Carol asks.

    Zosia exhales long and slow, leaning back against the couch. She’s thought about it over and over again to the point of sleep deprivation.

    “Best case scenario is that we are no longer partners.”

    “I can live with that,” Carol says easily, then pauses. “What’s the next scenario?”

    “Maybe one of us gets transferred departments.”

    Carol considers it. “I can also live with that. I could use a holiday at Cyber Crimes.”

    Zosia scoffs. “Oh, babe, you would not survive in Cyber Crimes. You barely know how to use your phone.”

    Carol places a hand over her chest, mouth open. “Okay, rude. I locked myself out one time…”

    They laugh but the lightness quickly dissolves when the next, more real scenario lingers between them, the one that Zosia hasn’t wanted to think about, the one that Carol is currently reading her mind about.

    “What if they move one of us?” Carol says.

    Yeah, that scenario. Different precinct, different daily life, at worst, different city. Zosia feels her throat go dry and she stares at the now dark TV screen.

    “It would suck,” she offers plainly and Carol has to laugh at that because maybe if she doesn’t, she might cry.

    “Is that your professional assessment?”

    “It would suck a lot.”

    Carol manages another laugh and Zosia shifts closer now, angling herself completely towards her, ankle tucked under the back of her knee and leg up on the couch. She reaches for her hands again, holding tight.

    “But I would rather not work with you at all than lose all this.” Zosia swallows back the lump in the throat and squeezes Carol’s fingers. “If being with you means I only get you in the evenings and on weekends, I will take it.”

    Zosia manages a smile now and Carol’s eyes glisten with the gentle threat of tears. “If it means we don’t have to worry anymore.” Another squeeze. “If it means I can focus on being your girlfriend instead of your Lieutenant every second of the day then that is a no brainer for me.”

    This time Carol turns, mirroring Zosia’s position. They both lean in at the same time, pressing their foreheads together, a single breath shared between them.

    “There is nothing more that I want than that, baby,” Carol whispers.

    Zosia closes her eyes and sighs, feeling every single emotion on the spectrum. A tear falls out of each eye and Carol wipes them away.

    When Zosia had moved to Albuquerque, she had not expected to find love in her workplace, let alone friendship and no less with someone, who on paper, is her subordinate.

    On top of that, she had not expected to find it with someone who would understand her the way that Carol Sturka does. It’s not just the grief, but it’s everything else; everything that makes her feel human and alive. Carol understands her in the way that someone who has stood in the same fire recognizes the shape of the burn.

    When Zosia thinks about all the things that led them here, that led her here to this exact moment, it is impossible to just simply name them because the truth is, it was a million tiny little things that, when she adds them all up, they just meant they were supposed to be together. And Zosia knew it. She knew it the very first time she touched her. It was like coming home, only to no home she had ever known. It was just a damn handshake in a cold and sterile briefing room and she knew it. It was like magic.

    “So, tomorrow.” Zosia settles back now so she can look Carol in the eyes.

    “Tomorrow,” Carol echoes.

    Tomorrow they will tell the Chief, tomorrow their professional lives might change, and tomorrow they will surrender control of whatever comes next.

    For Zosia, knowing that Carol is in this just as much as she is, is all that matters to her. It matters more than any title or assignment or precinct ever could.

     

     

     

    Chapter End Notes

    One more to go and I'm already a mess.

    Yes, I know this chapter was short but I promise the final one will be nice and long.

    All my love.

    Chapter 15: the liberation of love

    Chapter Summary

    Zosia fell first. Carol fell harder.

    Chapter Notes

    Thank you.

    "She wasn't doing a thing that I could see, except standing there, leaning on the balcony railing, holding the universe together."

    -J.D. Salinger

     

    *****

     

     

    “Detective Sturka and I are in a romantic relationship.”

    In the span of twelve hours, Carol has replayed this conversation in her head countless times. She had stared blankly up at the ceiling most of the night in bed next to Zosia and as much as Zosia had tried to pretend she was asleep, Carol could tell by the sound of her breathing and the tension in her jaw that she was very much just as awake as she was.

    Now they are here, nine in the morning, in Hank Schrader’s office delivering him news that may or may not send him into cardiac arrest. In all the different versions of this that Carol had played out, in none of them did he look quite so calm.

    “Hank,” Carol presses. “Say something.”

    Still, he remains unmoving and it is unsettling to say the least. Nothing but a tight lip and an unblinking, unreadable stare. Then he leans back in his chair, closes his eyes, laces his fingers on top of his head and lets out the heaviest and longest sigh Carol has ever heard.

    “For how long?” His eyes are still closed.

    “Does it matter?” Zosia says, leaning forward in her chair.

    The Chief opens his eyes now, studying the both of them intently for a moment too long. It is like he is going through his mental rolodex, trying to figure out what the signs were and how he missed them.

    “No,” he says finally. “I supposed it doesn’t.”

    Another silence stretches between them, just the noise and hustle from the bullpen muffled through the closed door. Carol shifts uncomfortably in her seat while her career flashes before her eyes and Zosia reaches over once, out of sight and under the desk, and lightly touches the inside of her wrist, enough to momentarily ground her.

    “So, what now?” Carol asks.

    A simple but loaded question, but that is what seems to rattle him. Trepidation edges into his eyes and he exhales sharply again, dropping his hands to the desk, the noise startling Carol.

    “I don’t know,” he admits and the honesty of it almost feels worse than anger or disappointment would. “I don’t know, guys. I really don’t know what I’m supposed to do here.”

    Carol swallows, feeling her own fear build deep within her stomach. She glances to Zosia who is already looking at her and if nothing else, she is not alone. She has Zosia.

    “You are my two best,” Schrader goes on but somehow the sentiment behind the words gets lost. “This department cannot afford to lose either of you but there are protocols. There are boundaries and lines and this- ” he gestures vaguely to the space between them. “This is very, very complicated.”

    Zosia reaches over again, this time her hand settling onto Carol’s knee. It stays there for a moment before she gives a squeeze and retracts her hand.

    Schrader shakes his head, scratching at his neck now visibly flushed. “I need to make some calls. Internal affairs, legal, my boss.” He stands, chair squeaking loudly. “You’re dismissed for now. Go on with your day as usual, I’ll be in touch.”

    —

    On the way to Zosia’s office, they pass by Manousos in the hallway. Zosia is a few steps ahead and he flashes Carol an intentionally uncharacteristic cheeky grin and thumbs up.

    “Y qué tal?”

    After all these years of knowing her, he chooses now to make a joke and he is very fortunate that Carol likes him as much as she does.

    Carol simply narrows her eyes at him and grabs his thumb.

    “Why don’t you take that and shove it up tu culo,” she whispers to him.

    Manousos clicks his tongue and pouts exaggeratedly slowly turning his thumb upside down.

    —

    It is impossible for Carol to go about her day “as usual” because there is nothing usual about it, not with this cloud looming over her head that hers and Zosia’s careers could be compromised.

    She reads witness statements and reports over and over without retaining a single word, she wanders into the break room for coffee but leaves empty handed, and at one point, she calls down to narcotics for a case update and halfway through the conversation she forgets what she is even calling about.

    “Um, Carol?” Detective Quinn asks on the other end of the line. “Everything okay?”

    “Uh-huh.”

    “You sure?”

    “Nope.”

    Detective Quinn laughs, but Carol does not. And by the time lunch rolls around, she does not even have the appetite to eat so she takes the elevator downstairs alone and heads outside to the parking lot for a breather.

    The autumn air is nippy as she leans against the brick wall, the wind slicing against her cheeks. She closes her eyes and tilts her head back and breathes out slowly in an attempt to untangle the knots in her chest.

    “Detective Sturka!” A voice calls.

    Carol squeezes her eyes tightly once, bracing herself, before opening them to see who on earth would be shouting her name like that, and that’s when she notices Officer Bailey standing beside a brand new squad car that she has never seen before. Is- is that a Tesla? He has her attention now though.

    “What on earth is that?” Carol asks and Officer Bailey’s face lights up immediately, as if he had been waiting to show off his new car to literally anybody.

    “Come here!” He waves her over.

    Carol is halfway to making up an excuse as to why she needs to go back inside but then she decides, fuck it, and a minute later she is in the driver’s seat while he explains from the passenger seat all the new and advanced features the car has, like three separate camera systems and automatic report integration.

    Officer Bailey is in the middle of showing her what all the different buttons and controls do when her phone buzzes in her pocket and it’s a text from Zosia.

    Where did you go?

    Quickly, she taps out a reply.

    Parking lot

    Beside her, this fully grown man is practically vibrating with excitement from all these different features. He encourages her to press any button she wants and one in particular she presses lights up the entire LED dashboard into a circus of flashing lights, like she is back at the Pinball Hall of Fame, and she startles.

    “Oh, Jesus.”

    “Right?”

    For the first time today, Carol finds herself not thinking about The Predicament, and as she is pressing more buttons like a toddler learning motor skills and hand-eye coordination for the first time, a presence sidles up to the open driver’s side window and Zosia stands there amused, with her hands in her pockets.

    “Do you like your police car, Carol?”

    Carol looks up and laughs, more than happy to see Zosia because she had just sort of disappeared without telling anyone.

    “Bailey kidnapped me.”

    “She came in here willingly, Lieutenant,” he says peering over from the passenger side. “She’s been pressing buttons for ten minutes.”

    Zosia snorts. “You know what Officer Bailey, I believe you.”

    Carol just shrugs and presses another button that activates the flashing lights on the roof, and Zosia entertains her, letting her press a few more buttons until she pulls a pack of cigarettes out of her pocket and gestures for Carol to follow.

    “Thanks, Bailey,” Carol says as she steps out of the car and Officer Bailey just nods, shooting her a finger gun.

    Carol follows at Zosia’s side as they round the corner to the back of the parking lot, shoes crunching against the gravel, as they perch up against the wall by the back stairwell door much like they did all those months ago under the star-speckled sky of a muggy June night.

    Zosia holds out the pack of Lucky Strikes to Carol and she slides one out before Zosia slides her own out, and she waits expectantly with the cigarette between her lips for it to be lit for her. Zosia edges closer, exactly the way she did that night, and sparks the lighter, flame whooshing in Carol’s face as the cigarette crackles to life.

    And as Zosia lights her own and takes a long drag, they both look to each other at the same, and as if they are reading each others minds, they burst out laughing. At the absurdity of it all, the journey, how they ended up here, and now the consequences they face. Simply just for falling in love.

    “Do you remember this night?” Carol asks in between giggles, blowing the smoke to her side where the wind carries it away.

    Zosia leans her shoulder against the wall and tucks the flapping stands of  hair behind Carol’s ear.

    “I thought about it for days after it happened, Carol. Of course I remember.”

    “You did? Even after the stakeout?”

    “Mhm. I got so nervous when I saw you standing there.”

    Carol takes another drag and nearly chokes on it. “Bullshit. You, nervous?”

    Zosia nods and her cheeks actually shade a light pink. She has put Carol through a mattress, knows every single inch of her naked body, what she tastes like, and exactly all the different sounds she makes when she orgasms but this, thinking about their first genuine interaction is what makes her blush.

    “Hard to believe, I know. I almost turned around and went back inside but you were already looking at me.”

    “Wow.” One corner of Carol’s mouth quirks up. “I made the great Lieutenant Zosia Nowak nervous.”

    “Very. And then you had the audacity to call me insufferable.”

    Carol snorts at that, eyes flicking to the ground. The memory of it all sits warm in her chest even through the tight knots.

    “You were,” she says, tugging at the sleeve of Zosia’s jacket, pulling her closer.

    “And now?”

    “Less.”

    Zosia smirks, rolling her eyes, then reaches forward to tilt Carol’s chin up so she can look at her, careful to not let the smoke waft into her face.

    “I think I fell in love with you then.”

    Carol feels that all the way in her toes. “I believe that.”

    The memory settles between them and they turn at the same time, backs against the wall. The sharp November breeze ruffles through their hair and the trees and together they finish their cigarettes, shoulder to shoulder.

    —

    It is 4:30pm when Carol and Zosia sit before the Chief again and Carol’s palms are slick against her jeans. Next to her, Zosia is unusually ramrod straight, bracing to hear the worst. Worst of all, Schrader looks older than he did this morning - eyes sunken, deep furrow in his brow - which tells Carol he comes bearing no good news.

    Carol can hear her own pulse beating in her ears and he does not waste one single second.

    “I spoke to everyone I needed to. Internal affairs, legal, my Captain- ” he pauses for a moment, running a hand down his face. “And this is not flexible.”

    Carol swallows hard and she might be the one going into cardiac arrest.

    “As per department policy and code of conduct, you cannot continue to work together. It’s a conflict of interest and there are chain of command issues, especially given that Zosia is technically in a position of authority over you, Carol.”

    They knew all that. They know all that. But hearing it out loud cements it into reality. Carol watches as Zosia goes still, even stiller than she already is and for one absolutely gut-wrenching moment, Carol thinks Zosia’s reputation might be ruined. At this time, she does not give a fuck about her own.

    “You will be reassigned a new partner,” the Chief says with finality looking directly at Carol. “He was recently promoted and has a good record. You will meet him on Monday.”

    Carol manages a nod but the thought of a new partner makes her stomach twist sideways because that means starting over again and having to build new trust. She wants to argue back; why can she not have Manousos again, but she knows she has no leverage right now so she bites her tongue and refocuses her attention to Zosia.

    “And you,” he says, now turning to Zosia. “You have a choice.”

    A choice? Carol perks up. She hadn’t realized options were on the table.

    “You can step down as Lieutenant and be reassigned as well, or…“ he hesitates like he already knows the answer and in both scenarios, him and the department loses. “The neighbouring precinct is in need of a Lieutenant and I put in a very good word for you so that position is yours, if you choose to take it. And I’ll be honest with you, Nowak, the pay is better than anything we can offer you here.”

    Carol’s head snaps towards Zosia so fast she almost gives herself whiplash. This is it. This is the best case scenario. The neighbouring precinct is only fifteen minutes away from here, and maybe twenty from Carol’s house and Zosia’s apartment. This is not exile by any means, it is hardly distance. This is so manageable.

    “Take it.”

    The words fly out of Carol’s mouth before she can even stop them because hell would completely freeze over before she would ever allow Zosia to even entertain taking a demotion. And she knows Zosia would not either.

    Zosia turns to Carol and everything in her body and face relaxes, but still she remains quiet and guarded.

    “Carol- ”

    “Zosia, it’s the best option.” Carol reaches over places her hand on Zosia’s arm, despite the Chief’s heavy gaze.

    Zosia puts a hand over Carol’s looks back at him. “Hank, we need a minute.” And Carol looks at her like she has lost her mind for not saying yes right away.

    The Chief sighs and looks at his watch and then he is already standing and shrugging on his coat.

    “It’s five o’clock,” he says picking up his briefcase. “Take the weekend to think it over but I need an answer first thing Monday morning because the changes will happen fast.” Then he pauses and he steps towards the door. “Whatever you decide, make sure you can both live with it.”

    And then he is out the door, leaving Carol and Zosia in the wake of his cigar breath.

    “Zosh,” Carol says with urgency once the Chief is gone and out of sight. “Why are you- ”

    “Baby.” Zosia cuts her off, leaning forward and placing a hand on Carol’s knee. She looks at her with soft and patient eyes. “Let’s talk about it later. Can I come over?”

    Carol huffs out a gentle breath, shoulders drooping. How could she ever argue with Zosia when she is looking at her like that.

    “You know you don’t need to ask.”

    —

    Carol sets two wine glasses on the counter with a sharp clink. Behind her, chicken thighs and vegetables are roasting in the oven. She looks up at Zosia who is standing in front of her with a gentle smile but watchful look on her face and for what feels like the first time, Carol is actually a little bit annoyed at her.

    “You’re taking the Lieutenant position, right?” Carol says it almost accusatory as she fills each glass with a heavy pour of Sauvignon Blanc. It’s more for herself than Zosia. “You are absolutely insane if you think you’re stepping down.” She pushes a glass towards her, bottom scraping across the granite as the wine sloshes against the rim.

    “Kochanie, please,” Zosia says, grabbing her hand instead of the glass. She tightens her grip when she can feel Carol trying to pull away. “Yes. Of course I am.”

    Carol jerks her head back at that, eyes immediately narrowing at Zosia. She had spent the last hour or so coming apart at the idea that Zosia was about the make the worst professional decision of her life and she knows her well enough to know that nobody would have gotten in the way of that.

    “What do you mean of course?”

    “Carol.” Zosia softens her tone, stepping closer slowly as if any sudden movement might scare her away. “Schrader sprung that on me so fast and I wasn’t expecting it and I just- I wanted to make sure we were on the same page. I knew right away I was going to take it but,” she pauses, eyes flickering over Carol’s face as if she were searching for something, “I really wanted to make the decision with you.”

    “Oh.” Carol unfurls now, nerves peeling away. Embarrassment creeps up on her but she can live with that because this is the part that really counts. They might not be partners at work anymore, but they are still partners in every other way that actually matters.

    “You know, you are so dense sometimes.” Zosia pulls Carol into a hug.

    Carol’s arms are still by her side but she flops her head against Zosia’s chest, forehead pressed against her collarbone and exhales through her nose.

    “I know,” Carol murmurs into the soft material of Zosia’s sweater. “I’m sorry.”

    “Don’t be.” Zosia voice is muffled into the top the Carol’s head, lost in the strands of her hair. “I love that you were so concerned.”

    Carol does not say anything, just wraps her arms around Zosia’s waist and they stand there like that until the oven beeps.

     

     

    *****

     

     

    Word travels fast on Monday. Not long after Zosia’s final meeting with the Chief the whispers and looks begin. By lunch time, the department is full on gossiping and Carol is mortified but she realizes quickly there is a fifty-fifty split reaction.

    Half her colleagues look and sound to be completely shocked to find out she has been “hooking up” with the Lieutenant, while everyone looks completely unfazed, as if they had known long before even Carol and Zosia did.

    That is confirmed when Detective Miller, someone that Carol hardly ever speaks to, walks past them in the bullpen and says ‘congrats, finally.’ Followed by Detective Kim bumping into Carol in the break room and saying ‘thanks you just won me fifty bucks.’

    The best reaction of all must be Rick’s and for the first time in Carol’s career, she actually finds herself halfway endeared to him.

    “Wow,” he exclaims as he absently ambles into Carol’s office where, of course, Zosia is there too sitting in a chair on the other side of Carol’s desk. He makes the motion and sound of his head exploding. “This is- I can’t believe this. This is so wonderful!”

    Carol wants to roll her eyes but she cannot bring herself to. She exchanges a glance with Zosia who looks like she might be suppressing a smile of her own.

    “I mean I just can’t believe I missed all the signs,” he continues while his smile grows wider and wider. “I guess I should have known when I saw you guys at the farmer’s market. Oh, and there was that time I saw you guys leaving work together and then arriving together again the next morning.”

    “Rick - ” Carol tries to cut him off, but he shows no signs of slowing.

    “Gosh, and all those times Carol went to Starbucks and came back with two drinks and Carol, you hate Starbucks.”

    Zosia whips her head around and looks at Carol as if to say ‘you hate Starbucks?’ to which Carol flashes her back a look that says ‘we’ll talk about it later.’

    “Rick.” Carol tries again.

    “And- and…”

    “Rick!” Carol and Zosia both shout at the same time and he finally shuts up. A blush creeps into his cheeks and he takes a breath.

    “Sorry,” he huffs. “I’m just happy for you ladies, is all.”

    Carol finally smiles at that. “Thanks, Rick.” And she means that sincerely.

    Then Rick’s face contorts, like something is just dawning on him. “I guess this is definitely not a sisterhood then, huh.”

    Zosia snorts. “Hm, definitely not.”

    “Right. My bad.” He runs a hand through his hair and then takes a step forward towards Zosia and sticks his hand out. “Well, Lieutenant, it was a pleasure working with you.”

    Carol watches at Zosia eyes his hand apprehensively for a second, but then she stands and shakes it.

    “Likewise, Detective.”

    And then Rick nods his head and disappears.

    —

    After lunch, while Zosia is completing her transfer paperwork with the Chief, Carol meets her new partner in the bullpen. His name is Kai Barrett. He is thirty-two years old and was just promoted from police officer last month. His skin is a smooth caramel colour and his smile is so eager and bright that it might blind the entire department.

    Carol tries and tries her best to smile at him in hopes that it does not come off as a wince because as much as she loathes having a new partner, she does not want this to start off on the wrong foot, but she also cannot have a partner who is miles behind her.

    “Carol Sturka,” she says, shaking his hand firmly.

    “Oh, I know,” Kai grins. “I’ve heard much about your legacy. It’s really an honour to get to work alongside you.”

    Oh. There it is again. This apparent legacy she has and still she wonders who is talking about her, especially because she feels like she has not done much of note this year because Nacho Varga and the Salamancas are still running around New Mexico freely.

    “I hope you’re a fast learner, Kai,” Carol says, tucking her hands into her pockets.

    “I’m always eager to learn.”

    Not exactly the answer she was hoping for, but nonetheless she will take it and swallow her pride for now.

    —

    By the middle of the afternoon, Zosia’s paperwork has been completed and now Carol is sitting in her almost empty office, while she watches Zosia clear the rest of her belongings.

    Already Zosia is starting her new job tomorrow; when the Chief said the changes were going to happen quickly, neither of them had anticipated this quickly. Now it feels too fast and too surreal but ultimately they had prepared for this.

    Through the open door and blinds, people are staring, watching their every interaction like they are catching up on missed episodes of a TV show. And while Carol can feel every single eyeball of her, she cannot be bothered anymore.

    Tomorrow she will have nothing to hide, tomorrow she will not have to tiptoe around the office like every can hear her inner thoughts. Tomorrow can she focus all her energy on hoping that Detective Kai Barrett is not a disappointment.

    “I hope the next Lieutenant is not as hot as you.” Carol smirks as she glances towards Zosia who is throwing the last of her belongings into a cardboard box.

    “I hope it’s a man for your sake,” Zosia responds without missing a beat.

    “My sake, or your sake?”

    “Yours.” Zosia meets her gaze as heated as ever, even despite the circumstance. “Maybe you’ll be a little less distracted.”

    “Oh, shut up.”

    Zosia merely nudges her in the shoulder and closes the lid on the box and with that, Carol feels one door of her life close with it and another open.

     

    +

     

    As Carol enters the bullpen the next morning with Manousos, she braces herself for just how difficult this adjustment is going to be. Not just for her personally, but for the department.

    Zosia’s office is still, obviously, empty when she walks by and she wonders who will be the next person to occupy it. And even though Carol had literally driven Zosia to work this morning because she has no new car yet, Carol still finds herself missing her presence.

    Kai is already in the break room making friends with Rick and Jeff and Carol thinks great, as if  Tweedledee and Tweedledum were not enough, now there might a third and it happens to be her partner. At least she still has Manousos.

    By the time the end of the week rolls around and Carol has exhausted every waking minute at work with Kai, she learns just how two completely different calibres him and Zosia are in every sense of the word.

    Kai is nice and friendly, but is he not Zosia. Kai drinks homemade green juice and does not know how Carol likes her coffee. Kai arrives in polo t-shirts and khaki pants and not perfectly tailored pant suits or crisp blouses. He does not smell like jasmine and citrus. Kai does not take notes during briefings and he is careless and clumsy at times, but Carol has enough grace for now to give him the benefit of the doubt and blame that on nerves. Carol does not let Kai drive.

    However, on the other hand, things with Zosia have not changed at all in spite of Carol’s overactive imagination. By the time the second week comes and goes, Zosia still stays over almost every night, save for the odd evening where she has a late night and then an early morning. And she has a new car now. Well, it’s the same actually. Another Ford Explorer only this time it is grey, not black, and a newer model.

    And Carol had not realized what a relief it would actually feel like to not work together anymore because now they do not bring their work home. Now there is a clear separation and a proper work-life balance and Carol is thankful that Zosia does not have nearly as much paperwork as she used to. The one saving grace about Kai is that because he is still so new and trying to impress, he has taken on the burden of processing most of the reports and case files, which in turn frees up more of Carol’s time so now their evenings and weekends are simply just theirs.

    So much so that on a Wednesday night, Carol takes Zosia on a date to a brewery in downtown Albuquerque that’s filled with too many hipsters with terrible moustaches, where they end up sitting too close to each other, drinking too many flights of beer and playing ping ping and cornhole all night; until they have to take an Uber home and they both show up to work the next morning a little late and very much hungover.

    So as a few more weeks pass, they have established a routine that works for them and then just like that is it Thanksgiving.

     

     

    *****

     

     

    “What’s burning?” Carol says the moment Manousos opens his front door and she is smacked in the face with the unmistakable smell of something on fire either in the oven or on the stove.

    Cooking and hosting has never been Manousos’ forte so colour Carol surprised when he invites her and Zosia, and Koumba and his new girlfriend over for Thanksgiving dinner.

    “Qué?” He says as he stands there with a hand on his hip, in his jeans, t-shirt, and yellow apron -  no doubt Javier’s - sassier than he has ever been. “Nothing is burning. What, you don’t say hello anymore?”

    Behind Carol, Zosia laughs as she holds a bottle of wine and a pecan pie that was purchased in haste at Sprouts on the way over here.

    “Something is definitely burning,” Zosia says, sniffing the air as they both step through the door and into the foyer.

    “Dios mío, nothing is - ”

    The smoke alarm goes off.

    From the living room, Javier can be seen sprinting to the kitchen and opening the oven which billows out a tuft of smoke before he runs to open the back door.

    “You were saying?” Carol says as she toes off her shoes.

    Manousos clicks his tongue and rolls his eyes. He takes the wine and pie from Zosia and brings it to the kitchen before coming back to take their jackets.

    The house is decorated to the nines, again no doubt the doing of Javier, with everything Thanksgiving themed. Wicker baskets of tiny pumpkins and gourds embellish the living room, bushels of apples clutter the kitchen, and once you can get past the smell of burning grease, you can almost smell a freshly baked peach cobbler.

    “Nice house,” Zosia says as her eyes wander around as the both of them follow Manousos into the kitchen. It is not nearly as big as Carol’s.

    “Thank you…” Manousos starts, then stops. “…Zosia.” He says her name slowly like the syllables are foreign to him because he has only ever called her Lieutenant. “This is my husband, Javi.” He gestures to Javier who is already releasing Carol from a long hug.

    “Hi, Zosia, it’s so nice to finally meet you. Manny used to complain about you all the time.” He winks as he holds out his hand for her.

    Beside him, Manousos flashes him a glare and Carol is stifling a laugh. She looks at Zosia whose eyes dart towards Manousos before darting back to Javier and she could be offended by that, but the twinkle in her eyes says otherwise.

    “So did Carol,” she answers. “Nice you meet you too. Thanks for having us over.”

    “Thank Manny. It was his idea.”

    For some time, while the smoke airs out, they chit-chat around kitchen counter about everything and nothing while Javier pours them each a mug of warm hard mulled cider as they wait for Koumba to arrive with his mysterious new girlfriend that he has been very coy about.

    “That is not what happened,” Manousos huffs when Zosia brings up a case they had briefly worked on together, before Zosia was assigned a partner.

    “Yes it is.”

    “No, it’s not.”

    “Yes it is, Manny. I was literally listening through the window.” Zosia folds her arms. “I took detailed notes.”

    “Then you must be deaf, blind, and illiterate.”

    Zosia scoffs, cocking her head all the way to the side. “Wow, you are so lucky I’m not your Lieutenant anymore.”

    Manousos waves his hand at her. “Not like it would have mattered. You were too busy starring in your own rom-com.”

    Zosia rolls her eyes so hard at him that they may actually get stuck in the back of her head and they keep going.

    In that moment, with no more professional boundaries to separate them, Carol quickly discovers something that she could have never foreseen and it is that Zosia and Manousos bicker like annoying siblings and honestly, she could not have asked for a better dynamic between them.

    So for a while, Carol hangs back and talks with Javier while she quietly watches her girlfriend and her best friend continue to argue about things that neither of them will ever win or lose. If there is one thing Carol is thankful for today, it is that Zosia is no longer an outsider in her life, but now firmly and undeniably a part of it as evidenced by whatever is unfolding before her eyes.

    Eventually the doorbell rings and because Carol is the one closest to the door, she goes to open it. She can see the shape of Koumba through the frosted glass and then a much shorter woman with long dark hair standing next to him.

    The moment she opens the door, she is met the one and only…

    Fucking Laxmi.

    —

    “Holy shit.”

    Carol does not mean for the words to come out as loud and surprised as they do, but there are about a million other people she had envisioned Koumba with before Laxmi would have even crossed her mind. Now she understands why he had been so secretive about it.

    “Well, Happy Thanksgiving to you too, Carol.”

    “Play nice, ladies,” Koumba says, ushering in Laxmi before himself.

    Carol stands stock-still with her mouth halfway open as Laxmi shimmies past her and towards the kitchen where Manousos and Zosia are both looking at her like they have seen a ghost.

    “Hello, old friends.” Laxmi says it like she has a chip on her shoulder as she sets a bottle of champagne on the counter, although she had barely known Zosia before her and Schrader had let her go.

    Zosia says nothing at first and Carol thinks this might be the first time she has ever seen her uncomfortable in a social setting. Manousos, on the other hand, looks like he has a dictionary of things he wants to say but is biting his tongue.

    “Yes, yes, I know you all have questions,” Koumba says, putting his arm around Laxmi. “But let’s save that for another time and just enjoy tonight, oui?”

    A collective silence falls upon the room for a moment until Javier turns the music up and then all the tension is dissolved.

    Carol drags Zosia to the living room and they settle closely together on the couch with a glass of wine each, where Zosia drapes an arm around Carol’s shoulder. Carol leans in to kiss her on the mouth when she thinks no one is looking and it lingers for a moment and Carol wants so badly to deepen it and just as she thinks she might when she feels Zosia’s lips move against hers, the sound of someone clearing their throat startles them apart and the both of them flush bright red when Laxmi is standing right there, overflowing glass of champagne in hand.

    “Koumba told me about this.” Laxmi gestures vaguely at them, as she takes a seat in the arm chair.

    “Laxmi,” Carol starts, trying to control her face. “Whatever inappropriate comment you want to make - ”

    “No.” She interrupts, shaking her head and holding a hand up. “It’s… good for you, Carol.” She sips her champagne. “It’s nice to see you…not miserable.”

    Carol, at first, is not quite sure how to take that. She can hear the bite in it even if Laxmi is trying to come off as sincere. But truthfully, she had never actually disliked Laxmi, she had just been a poor detective, or rather, detective work was not meant for her and Carol’s ego got in the way of that many times and she often took it out on Laxmi in ways she wishes she had not. And now here she is, back in Carol’s life, but in what capacity, she has no idea. So she does as Koumba tells her to and plays nice.

    “Um, thank you, Laxmi,” Carol mutters and Zosia squeezes her arm.

    “So…” Zosia tries now. “What have you been up to?”

    “Hmm, what have I been up to?” Laxmi looks up to the ceiling and taps her finger against her chin. “Well, I will say you and Hank firing me was one of the best things that’s ever happened to me.”

    “Oh?” Zosia says glancing at Carol.

    It’s a trap. Carol places her hand on Zosia’s knee as her way of warning her.

    “No, Zosia.” Laxmi rolls her eyes. There it is. “I’m a fucking shift supervisor at Best Buy. Koumba came in one day to buy a PlayStation and that’s when we reconnected.”

    Zosia opens her mouth to say something, but Koumba appears from the kitchen before she can get a word out.

    “Ma cherie,” he says reaching for her hand. “Come help me set the table.”

    Once him and Laxmi have disappeared, both Carol and Zosia let out a huge breath, falling into each other in a quiet fit of laughter, and Zosia presses a soft kiss into Carol’s ear.

    “Come on,” Zosia says. “Let’s go see if Manny and Javi need help.”

    —

    Despite the uncomfortable encounter with Laxmi; and Manousos nearly burning the turkey along with his entire house, the rest of the night turns out to be lovelier than Carol had expected.

    She stands in the kitchen as Manousos curses in Spanish while he tries to salvage a tray of stuffing that looks a little bit too dark to be intentional. Javier laughs it off because otherwise he might cry. Koumba goes around topping up everyone’s drinks and Carol watches as Zosia moves around with ease, as if she has been friends with these people for years.

    Dinner gets served on an orange and brown checkered table cloth lined with candles, fake leaves, and a cornucopia centrepiece. Manousos insists that the stuffing is not ruined, the edges are just crisp and he is right. The turkey is also not as burnt as expected, and the gravy which had started out as a thick goop because Carol had spilled too much flour in it, turns out perfect after she dumps half a bottle of red wine in it.

    Conversation laps in easy waves, glasses clink, and much to Laxmi’s horror, Koumba tells the story about how he realized he was in love with the woman in the astronaut blue polo shirt when he saw her standing between air fryers and toaster ovens.

    Carol finds herself actually sitting and enjoying the company rather than wishing and waiting for an excuse to leave. Next to her, Zosia finds her hand under the table and their fingers lace together in Carol’s lap.

    Zosia squeezes once, tightly, and Carol realizes she had not expected to get this feeling back. Not just the love, she knows that by now, but this sort of evening where the warmth does not feel fragile or temporary.

    Holidays had become something that felt distant for Carol, something she associated more with absence than presence. They were merely something she had to get through and not something she felt.

    So when Carol squeezes her hand back and Zosia looks over to her and smiles, Carol feels the pressure building behind her eyes and has to blink it away.

    Maybe this is what to means to come back to something.

    —

    Later that night, they fall into bed with full bellies and soft pajamas. Carol props her head against the headboard as she reads a book, while Zosia is tucked around her, head on her chest already half asleep to the rise and fall of Carol’s breath. Softly, Carol’s fingers scrape against the nape of Zosia’s neck as her eyes struggle to follow the words on the page.

    Not even ten minutes later, the book has fallen out of Carol’s hand and they have both dozed off, lamp still on, glasses still on.

     

     

    *****

     

     

    Four days after Thanksgiving, Zosia gets a phone call early in the morning, long before the sun even makes an appearance. Carol wakes first to the sound of the buzzing on the nightstand and when she peers up and over Zosia, she sees Marek’s name on the screen.

    Quickly, she nudges her awake and Zosia answers in a groggy panic because Marek would never call at this hour unless it was an emergency. It turns out her father has suddenly fallen extremely ill and is in the hospital with a poor prognosis and the entire Nowak family is requesting her presence back in Gdańsk as soon as possible because time is already running out.

    Carol holds her tightly while she cries and Zosia tells her through broken sobs that she had only just repaired a very strained relationship with him a few years ago and had planned to go home sooner to see him but never found the time. And now, this. For a while they stay like that. Carol scoots in behind Zosia on the bed, legs at her sides and wraps her arms around her, hugging her until the tears subside.

    It is still pitch black when Carol glances out the window where the cold December air presses against the pane.

    “Get dressed,” she says softly, rubbing Zosia’s back under the cotton of her t-shirt. She knows that sleep will not come back for either of them. “I’ll take you back to your apartment so you can start packing.”

    Zosia agrees and fifteen minutes later they are on the road. While they are at the apartment, Carol searches for flights while Zosia frantically and distractedly throws whatever she can into a suitcase.

    “The next flight is tonight at five,” Carol says.

    “Nothing sooner?” Zosia asks, peering her head out of the bedroom.

    “Nothing.”

    “Okay.”

    By eight o’clock, Carol has already emailed Schrader telling him she will not make it in today due to an emergency and Zosia is on the phone with her own Chief explaining her situation. Luckily, her Chief is more than sympathetic and grants her a two week leave so Carol books the tickets for her.

    The rest of the morning is spent at the apartment while Zosia anxiously paces around and gets her travel documents in order. Carol does her best to keep her grounded by turning on Never Been Kissed in the background and it works, kind of, but Zosia’s head is elsewhere and Carol does not blame her.

    By two o’clock, they are back on the road and en route to the airport. Carol holds Zosia’s hand the entire way.

    The air is cold and brisk when they arrive to the international departures terminal and a chill runs down Carol’s spine as she steps out of the car. She takes Zosia’s suitcase out of the trunk while she waits against the passenger door, under the Lufthansa sign. It only hits her then how much she is going to miss her.

    “Hey,” Carol says gesturing towards Zosia’s phone as she rolls the suitcase in front of her. “Did you check your seat number?”

    “No?” Zosia pulls her phone out of her pocket and opens her electronic boarding pass and scans over the details. As she does, Carol watches her, watches the way her brow furrows with confusion then realization.

    “2C? Carol, that’s—“

    “Business class.”

    Zosia’s eyes widen. “You- you booked me a business class seat?”

    Her lips curve up and it is the first time all day Carol has seen her smile and she breathes a sigh of relief at that.

    “Only the best for my girl.” And she means that. It feels like the least she can do is send her girlfriend off in style and comfort while she travels to see her dying father.

    “Kochanie, I don’t know what to say,” Zosia says, pulling Carol into her arms. “Thank you. Thank you.”

    They hug there for a long minute, bodies pressed tightly together, while cars honk at them to hurry up and move but it is just background noise. Carol buries her face into Zosia’s neck, breathing her in as if she were never coming back. She places a soft kiss there and when she pulls back and looks at Zosia, she is crying again. Carol reaches up with both hands, thumbing away the tears.

    “I’m only a phone call away,” Carol reassures her. “Call me at any time. I don’t care how late or early it is.”

    Zosia nods, hands fisted into the material of Carol’s jacket and chin quivering because she is going to miss her just as much. Then Carol gently angles her face down and kisses her salt stained lips. One. Twice. Three times.

    “I love you.” Zosia’s voice trembles and Carol chest feels like cinderblocks. She fights her own urge to cry because she knows if she starts, she will not stop and Zosia is already a mess.

    “I love you,” Carol says. “Now go before I start crying too.”

    Reluctantly they let go of each other. Zosia turns, suitcase in hand and heads into the airport. Carol watches until she’s through the sliding doors and Zosia turns back around once, waves, and then disappears into the crowd.

    With that, Carol is left alone with the awful realization that Zosia will be gone for ten whole days.

    She fucking hates that.

    —

    Later that night Carol is alone in her now very quiet and very empty house. She cannot even hear the refrigerator buzzing, only the sound of her own self breathing so she decides to pour herself a drink. Scotch tonight because she is feeling sad so why not make herself sadder. When she looks at her liquor cabinet though, the bottles have remained mostly untouched. Six months ago, she would have gone through a whole bottle of whiskey in a week, tops. She smiles at that, not realizing the impact Zosia has had on that part of her life. When the photo of Helen catches her eye, she wishes she could tell her.

    Carol settles onto the couch and pulls a blanket over her lap but does not bother to turn on the TV. Instead, she takes out her laptop and starts tracking Zosia’s flight and it occurs to her then that ever since they first kissed at Kenny’s retirement party, they have hardly gone one whole day without seeing each other.

    The fear had been there that working at different precincts might have wedged some distance between them, perhaps with Zosia making new friends, different work loads, and going about her day differently, but if anything it has only made them closer and their relationship stronger.

    As the burn of the Scotch tingles at Carol’s lips, she glances over her shoulder at the door like some helpless dog waiting for its owner to come home and notices a pair of Zosia’s shoes that sit on the shoe rack, illuminated through the stained glass by the porch light and she pouts.

    Christ. You’re being so fucking dramatic.

    These are going to be the longest ten days of her life.

     

    +

     

    Carol does what she does best. She busies herself at work. Her and Kai take on as many cases as they can and by the second day, Carol decides that she is finally going to give Kai a proper chance instead of being miserable around him and brushing off him off. She is going to take him under her wing because if there is one thing that meeting and being with Zosia has taught her, it is to give people a chance.

    By the end of the fourth day, Kai has actually impressed her. He is much sharper and more intelligent than she had given him credit for and he is actually quite funny. Not as funny as her, of course, but he has offered her a laugh or two when she needed it.

    —

    That evening while Carol is already tucked into bed, Zosia calls. She has called every morning and every night despite the timezones and Carol has waited with bated breath for each one. Two days ago, Zosia’s father was put on life support and now Zosia tells her with a devastating waver in her voice that tomorrow they will probably have to take him off it. It pains Carol that she cannot physically be there with her. All she wants to do is reach through the phone and hold her.

    When the conversation gets too heavy, Zosia insists that Carol tells her about her day.

    “Kai led his first investigation today,” Carol says.

    “Oh wow,” Zosia huffs into the speaker. “You actually let him? I’m impressed.”

    “I figured it was time.”

    “What changed your mind?”

    Carol puts the phone on speaker and places it on Zosia’s pillow, then rolls onto her side. She shrugs as if Zosia can see her. “I just thought about you and what might have happened if I had never given you a chance.”

    “Hm, so what are you saying?” There’s a hint of amusement in Zosia’s voice now. “You have a secret massive crush on him and your only way of flirting is to give him a hard time?”

    Carol scoffs loudly at that and the way she just had to emphasize massive, but Zosia is absolutely not wrong; that was exactly how Carol had behaved.

    “Yes,” she deadpans. “How did you know? Listen, I have some bad news, I don’t think we’re going to work out…”

    That finally earns a real and genuine laugh from Zosia and Carol hadn’t realized how much she missed the sound, and it sounds like Zosia needed that. The moment stays light for a little while longer until Carol hears the voice a distressed woman in the background speaking Polish.

    “Baby, I have to go,” Zosia says. “We are heading back to the hospital now.”

    Carol takes the phone off speaker and puts it to her ear now because she wants to be as close to Zosia as she can. “Okay. Call me if anything happens. You know my ringer is on.”

    “I will. Love you.”

    “Love you too.”

    Carol falls asleep hugging Zosia’s pillow.

    —

    The fifth day is a Saturday and instead of dragging her feet around the house, Carol does something she should have done a long, long time ago. Long before she had even met Zosia. She heads to Lowe’s and buys a bunch of cardboard boxes and when she is back home, she brings them upstairs and stands in her closet. She closes her eyes for a moment and takes a deep breath that starts all the way low in her abdomen and slowly blows it out of her mouth.

    She is going to donate Helen’s clothes.

    Approximately two hours and several shed tears later, the boxes are full, the closet is about fifty percent less full, and Carol is one hundred percent much lighter. One by one, she lugs the four boxes downstairs and into the trunk of her car and drives to Goodwill.

    As she stands there and stares at the boxes stacked next to the donation bin, Carol expects herself to feel much sadder. She is waiting for the tears to come again but they never arrive. She is waiting for her chest to crack into million pieces but it never does.

    Instead, what she feels is certainty.

    The first thing Carol does when she gets home again is pull out all the clothes that Zosia has crammed into one of her drawers and hangs them up in the closet instead. There is not much, but there is enough and it is a start.

    When she is done, she heads downstairs to make dinner because it is already after six o’clock. As she walks through her house, it is impossible to not notice all the ways that Zosia has already left a mark here.

    It starts in the kitchen where there is a San Francisco 49ers mug left in the drying rack that Zosia had brought over one morning because she did not have a proper travel mug; Carol had called her unhinged for driving with an un-lidded ceramic mug. It lives there now. Then next to the sink there is a small jar of coarse salt that Carol does not remember buying occupying a space where there was previously nothing.

    Then in the living room there is a pair of Zosia’s socks wedged between the couch cushions that Carol has not bothered to remove and a hair tie that is looped around the remote control.

    The bookshelf is the worst, but Carol’s favourite. There are books that Zosia has brought over, slotted in between her own with no regard for genre or author, or any system really. Zosia had told her once she does not believe in systems, just proximity. ‘These two would hate each other’ she had said, placing them side by side and Carol has not moved them since.

    Even the silence feels different so as she stands in her kitchen thinking of what to eat, she tells herself that all these things could be undone in a moment if she wanted them to be.

    She does not want to move a thing.

    —

    On the sixth day is when Zosia’s father finally passes. Carol’s phone rings at 5:12am and she already knows before she has even picked it up.

    She sits with Zosia on the phone for a long while. She offers kind and patient words, but mostly they sit in silence with unpleasant but familiar hospital sounds in the background that make Carol’s stomach churn while she allows Zosia to feel whatever it she needs and wants to feel.

    “And when the lease on your apartment is up in the spring, I- I don’t want you to renew it.”

    Carol does not expect the tears to spring from Zosia’s eyes the way they do, like a dam that has just broken, years of emotions spilling out all at once. She is quick to thumb them away, Zosia’s face now softly cradled in Carol’s hands. Zosia looks at her in a way that answers every single unasked question and unspoken word that has ever lived inside of Carol.

    “So…is that a yes?”

    “Yes,” Zosia chokes out in a sob that is also a laugh. She reaches forward and pulls Carol by the hips until her legs have come apart and wrapped around her like a koala hugging a tree. Then she tucks her face all the way into Carol’s neck. “God, yes.”

    It’s then, with the both of them wrapped around each other like that was always how it was supposed to be, that the last ten years of Carol’s life flash before her. All the grief, all the misplaced anger, every blindingly drunken day and night that left her hugging porcelain and regretting every single life decision that had led her to that point; wishing and wishing for someone to come back that was never going to.

    And on top of that, nobody telling her that falling in love with someone else would make her feel so goddamn guilty—

    But then, as Zosia lifts her head, face completely soaked and pressing a kiss onto Carol’s cheek, then the other, then on her chin, Carol can see the next ten years of her life and more. It is brighter, it is warmer. There is hope, and there is simply something to look forward to.

    “Can we get a dog?” Zosia blubbers through the tears.

    “Yes baby, two even.” Carol starts crying now and when Zosia’s hand comes up to settle warmly on her chest over her heart, it only makes her cry more. “And you can have your garden.”

    “I love you, Carol.”

    “I love you too, Zosia.”

    —but in that same breath, nobody tells Carol that it would also be so liberating.

    And in that, she finds freedom.

     

     

    end.
